Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in, I really appreciate it. 

Um, well, it's probably nothing too serious, but I actually ended up calling the EAP at work to see someone. I supervise a sales team, so the office is always pretty lively, which is usually fine, but... lately, I guess I've been a little on edge? Like, every little bang in the office just really gets to me. If someone just shuts a file drawer, or... or drops a coffee mug, I just jump so hard. I mean, quiet places are supposed to be relaxing, right? \n\nI guess sometimes if it's *too* quiet, my mind might wander a bit, just thinking about work or what I need to do next week. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, specific situations? Well, I guess... I mean, the office can be a little distracting lately. 

It's not that I'm anxious, really. It's just, you know, as a supervisor, I like to be aware of my surroundings, right? So, lately, when I'm at my desk, I find myself just... keeping an eye out. Like, I'll check where my boss is, or I'll notice where the exits are, or I'll kind of glance over my shoulder to see if anyone is walking up behind me. Even when I'm supposed to be looking at a report or helping my team. 

But that's just being observant, isn't it? It's good to know what's going on in your workspace, right? I just like to be prepared and stay on top of things. It doesn't mean I'm on edge, I'm just... being thorough. That's a good quality for a supervisor to have, don't you think?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, most of the day? No, I wouldn't say that at all. 

I mean, sure, there are moments during the day where things get a little busy or stressful, but that's just normal life, right? Everyone has parts of their day where they have to focus a bit more or juggle a few things. But I definitely don't walk around feeling anxious from morning till night. 

Like I said, I have my moments of just being observant at work, but outside of that, I'm pretty relaxed. I can still sit down and watch a game or grab dinner without feeling wound up. I think I handle the day-to-day stuff pretty well, honestly. It's totally normal to not feel perfectly calm every single second of the day, isn't it? I think I'm doing a pretty good job managing it all.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, I guess I sort of mentioned this earlier, but... I don't really see it as looking for *threats*, exactly. That sounds a little extreme, doesn't it? 

But, yeah, like I said before about the office... I do find myself kind of keeping track of things a bit more now. Like, I'll catch myself checking where my boss is sitting, or just making a mental note of where the exits are. And sometimes I'll just glance over my shoulder to see if someone's walking up behind me. Even when I'm supposed to be totally focused on helping my team with their numbers or whatever. 

But I feel like that's just being a responsible supervisor, you know? It's good to be aware of your environment, right? I'm just trying to make sure everything is running smoothly. It's not like I'm paranoid or anything. I just like to be on top of things. That's a totally normal way to work, isn't it?

Um, no, not really. With my family and friends, I'm... I'm pretty much myself.

I mean, I still go to work every day, and I still go to the store and all my normal spots. \n\nI guess... well, I try to stay away from the breakroom at work if it's really crowded and noisy, but I think that's just because I like to have a quiet lunch, you know? It's not like I'm scared to go in there or anything. I just prefer to eat at my desk where I can catch up on emails and be productive. That's a pretty normal thing for a supervisor to do, right? Just trying to stay focused and not get distracted by all the chatter? I definitely wouldn't say I'm avoiding anything, just... managing my time well, I hope?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully relax and calm down when you're not at work or in a potentially noisy environment?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I think so. I mean, when I get home, I really try to just kick back. \n\nI guess sometimes it takes a little bit to wind down after a long day, but that's just because I'm thinking about, you know, making sure my team is set up for tomorrow. But once I'm settled in, I feel like I can relax just fine. I might keep the TV volume a little low just so it's peaceful, but that's just because I like a calm environment, right? It's good to have a quiet space to recharge. So yeah, I'd say I can relax pretty well. I'm doing the right things to take care of myself, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Um, intense fear? No, I don't think I've ever had anything like that. I mean, I've been startled before, like if someone drops something heavy or a car honks too loud, but that's just a normal reaction, isn't it? \n\nI definitely haven't had any sudden, overwhelming panic or anything that peaks like you're saying. So, no, I wouldn't say I've ever felt anything like what you're describing. I'm doing pretty well with keeping things in perspective, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge even on your days off from work?\n\nPatient: Um, on my days off? No, not really. Keeping active is a good way to spend a day off, isn't it? It means I'm not just sitting around doing nothing. So, yeah, I feel pretty normal on my days off. I think I balance things out okay, don't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge in completely quiet and safe environments?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's a healthy way to unwind, don't you think? Just enjoying the peace and quiet? So, no, I definitely don't feel anxious when I'm just hanging out in a safe space. I'm pretty good at leaving work at work, you know?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific situations or things that make you feel more on edge or anxious?\n\nPatient: Um, specific situations? Um, well, like I was saying, it's really just... mostly at work, I guess.

Um, no, not constantly. Like I mentioned, it's really not like that. 

I guess I just... I mean, if I'm at the grocery store or something, I'm just getting my groceries, you know? I'm not scanning the aisles for danger. That would be crazy. I'm just a normal guy doing normal things. 

Like I said before, maybe I just pay a little more attention to where the doors are if it's super crowded, but that's just being safe. It's not like a constant, obsessive thing. I'm definitely not walking around terrified all the time. I'm doing okay, right? It sounds like I'm doing okay.

If things get a little stressful, I just take a deep breath and handle it. That's the best way to deal with things, right? Just keeping a level head? So, no, I wouldn't say I've ever felt anything like what you're describing. I'm doing pretty well with keeping things in perspective, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge even on your days off from work?\n\nPatient: Um, on my days off? No, not really. I mean, weekends are my time to just, you know, decompress. \n\nI guess sometimes I might feel a little... keyed up on a Saturday morning, but that's just because I'm used to being on the go all week, right? It takes a second to shift gears from being a supervisor to just being at home. But I wouldn't call it being on edge or anxious. I just like to stay busy, maybe tackle some chores or run errands. Keeping active is a good way to spend a day off, isn't it? It means I'm not just sitting around doing nothing. So, yeah, I feel pretty normal on my days off. I think I balance things out okay, don't you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge in completely quiet and safe environments?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, quiet places are supposed to be relaxing, right? \n\nI guess sometimes if it's *too* quiet, my mind might wander a bit, just thinking about work or what I need to do next week. But that's just being proactive, isn't it? It's not like I'm sitting there feeling nervous or anything. I actually really appreciate having a quiet house to myself. It's a healthy way to unwind, don't you think? Just enjoying the peace and quiet? So, no, I definitely don't feel anxious when I'm just hanging out in a safe space. I'm pretty good at leaving work at work, you know?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific situations or things that make you feel more on edge or anxious?\n\nPatient: Um, specific situations? Well, I guess... I mean, the office can be a little distracting lately. \n\nIt's not that I'm anxious, really. It's just, you know, as a supervisor, I like to be aware of my surroundings, right? So, lately, when I'm at my desk, I find myself just... keeping an eye out. Like, I'll check where my boss is, or I'll notice where the exits are, or I'll kind of glance over my shoulder to see if anyone is walking up behind me. Even when I'm supposed to be looking at a report or helping my team. \n\nBut that's just being observant, isn't it? It's good to know what's going on in your workspace, right? I just like to be prepared and stay on top of things. It doesn't mean I'm on edge, I'm just... being thorough. That's a good quality for a supervisor to have, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're anxious or on edge for most of the day, regardless of what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Um, most of the day? No, I wouldn't say that at all. \n\nI mean, sure, there are moments during the day where things get a little busy or stressful, but that's just normal life, right? Everyone has parts of their day where they have to focus a bit more or juggle a few things. But I definitely don't walk around feeling anxious from morning till night. \n\nLike I said, I have my moments of just being observant at work, but outside of that, I'm pretty relaxed. I can still sit down and watch a game or grab dinner without feeling wound up. I think I handle the day-to-day stuff pretty well, honestly. It's totally normal to not feel perfectly calm every single second of the day, isn't it? I think I'm doing a pretty good job managing it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're often on the lookout for potential threats or dangers, even when there's no obvious reason to be?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess I sort of mentioned this earlier, but... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, not really. I mean, home is home, right? It's my place. 

I guess... well, I always lock the doors, obviously. Everyone does that. And maybe I double-check them before I go to bed, just to be sure. But I'm not, like, peeking through the blinds or pacing the halls or anything crazy like that. I just watch TV or play some video games, you know? Just regular stuff to unwind. 

If I hear a weird noise outside, I might mute the TV for a second just to listen, but that's just common sense, isn't it? Better safe than sorry. It doesn't mean I'm scared or anything. I just like to make sure everything is secure so I can relax. That's pretty normal, right? I feel like most guys my age would do the exact same thing.

It's totally normal to not feel perfectly calm every single second of the day, isn't it? I think I'm doing a pretty good job managing it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're often on the lookout for potential threats or dangers, even when there's no obvious reason to be?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess I sort of mentioned this earlier, but... I don't really see it as looking for *threats*, exactly. That sounds a little extreme, doesn't it? \n\nBut, yeah, like I said before about the office... I do find myself kind of keeping track of things a bit more now. Like, I'll catch myself checking where my boss is sitting, or just making a mental note of where the exits are. Even when I'm supposed to be totally focused on helping my team with their numbers or whatever. \n\nBut I feel like that's just being a responsible supervisor, you know? It's good to be aware of your environment, right? I'm just trying to make sure everything is running smoothly. It's not like I'm paranoid or anything. I just like to be on top of things. That's a totally normal way to work, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're on edge or anxious when you're interacting with your family or close friends?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's totally fine. \n\nI think I'm a pretty relaxed guy to be around, honestly. I definitely don't want to be one of those people who brings their work stress home and takes it out on their family, you know? That's not me at all. I do a good job of keeping things separate, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your anxiety or being on edge is mostly related to your work environment or does it happen in other areas of your life as well?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was saying, it's really just... mostly at work, I guess. \n\nI mean, I don't even really like calling it \"anxiety\" or being \"on edge.\" That makes it sound like I'm falling apart, and I'm really not. I'm just... staying alert, you know? Keeping an eye on the office, making sure the team is good, knowing who is coming and going. That's just being a good supervisor. \n\nOutside of work, like I said, I'm fine. If I'm out at a restaurant or a bar with buddies, sure, I might glance around and notice where the exits are, but that's just common sense, right? It's not like I'm sitting there sweating or freaking out. I'm just being smart about my surroundings. Everyone does that a little bit, don't they? I feel like I'm handling everything pretty well, all things considered.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being constantly on the lookout for potential threats or dangers, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not constantly.
